# GATE-SPEC-B: gates Q01 to Q28 seen from the implementation

First written on 2026-11-13 (UTC) between 18:50 and 19:13 from the code, tests and data in the working tree (HEAD 46d61b8f at the start, b84e3f7b at the last read). Re-verified and brought up to date on the same day from 19:18 to about 20:00 UTC against the working tree (HEAD cd832f28 when that pass began, b2911abf during its node suite, 9b6567cb at its last edit): section 8 lists what that pass checked, what it changed and what it did not re-check. The tree is shared and keeps changing, so every statement is true of the files at the time named; `spec-b-evidence/facts.py` and `verify_quotes.py` recompute the checkable ones. Companion to GATE-SPEC-A.md (the 28 gates defined from the governing text; another engineer's file, in the tree since 19:46 UTC and not edited here). This file describes what exists; it defines no pass rule, sets no `passes` flag and lowers nothing. Where a number, title or path is given it was read from the file or produced by a run named in section 7. Fourth pass: 2026-11-14 from 00:09 UTC (HEAD c7f464a1 at its first run). The container had restarted again (uptime 4 minutes at 00:09) and the third pass had been cut off while writing: it had updated sections 1, 2 and 3 but not the gate sections of section 4, which still carried the state of 20:00 UTC on 13 November, and the locked job it referred to had never run. This pass finished that work and went on from it. New: section 2.5 (the history layer's data contract read for the gates), the three owner additions OA10 to OA12 (969 sub-requirements now, not 966; 5.3), and sections 7.5, 7.6, 8.5 and 8.6. Every gate whose inputs moved has an `Updated at pass 4` bullet in section 4 (Q21 was rewritten in place, and statements of Q02, Q06, Q08, Q16 and Q22 were corrected in place; 8.5 lists them); the gates whose inputs did not move say so, with the evidence. The quotation check `spec-b-evidence/verify_quotes.py` had searched a corpus that contained this very file, so its result at the first three passes proved nothing; it was rewritten and re-run (8.5). A statement about a file that other workstreams were still editing (`app/data/vegetation.json`, the `tools/bt/photomatch/` files, `kit/evidence/`, the correction files) is true of the time it names. Fifth pass: 2026-11-15 from 22:53 UTC (HEAD 064a8bec at its start, 7d79b912 during its node suite), the fifth start of the campaign. Every workflow had ended together at about 01:39 UTC on 14 November at the weekly limit, the container had restarted again (up 2 minutes at 22:53) and the other workstreams began rewriting their files from about 22:56 on, so some figures below are of a file that changed while this was read and say so. This pass did not redo the fourth: it compared the key inputs of every gate with the commit that holds the fourth pass (`spec-b-evidence/pass5/inputs_delta.py`; 3.1), re-ran the light checks twice (at the start and at the end of the pass) and one locked job (7.7), re-read the files that moved, wrote an `Updated at pass 5` bullet into each of the 28 gate sections and added 3.1 (what moved), 3.2 (entry points with their measured cost), 3.3 (calls that were tried), 7.7, 8.7 and 8.8; the summary table and 2.1 to 2.4 were corrected in place (8.7).

Sixth pass: 2026-11-16 from 02:30 UTC (HEAD 536b7773 at its first run; the container had restarted at about 02:20, `uptime` said up 10 minutes at 02:30:56; the owner's full pause of 01:53 had been lifted at 02:32 by the orders recorded as DEC-OWNER-2026-11-16). What changed since the fifth pass is mostly one thing: **gate runners now exist**. `tools/bt/gates/` (34 tracked files) and `tests/governance/gates/` (36) hold executable runners for 11 of the 28 gates (Q01 to Q06, Q14, Q19, Q20, Q23 and Q25) with 585 unit tests, a mutation check of three of them (48 of 48 injected defects caught) and seven stored result files in this folder (`Q01.json`, `Q02.json`, `Q14.json`, `Q19.json`, `Q20.json`, `Q23.json`, `Q25.json`), every one with the verdict FAIL, for reasons that, as far as the failing items say, are the product's and the data's and not the runner's (the code of the runners was not audited here: 8.11). Section 2.6 describes the runners, their tests and what does not exist; section 3.0 is the one-page table of runner state per gate; every gate section of section 4 has an `Updated at pass 6` bullet; 3.1 lists what else moved. Statements of the earlier passes that say no gate runner exists (2.1) are true of the time they name and are superseded. New in the surroundings: the owner's orders of 16 November (no budget cap, so Q27 becomes a report; the 47 conflicts to be resolved by research, so the conflict inputs of Q02, Q04 to Q06, Q19, Q22 and Q28 will move; the owner's PC attached through Remote Control, which the session lists as a computer whose folder cannot be attached, so Q15 and the real-GPU parts of Q10 and Q24 stay NOT RUNNABLE HERE), and seven findings about the runners themselves (2.6 and 8.9: among them the `-I` import failure of two runners and the one-entry difference in counting unmarked register entries). What was run is in 7.8 and what was checked and corrected in 8.9 to 8.11. Nothing else of this file's contract changed: it describes what exists, defines no pass rule, sets no `passes` flag and lowers nothing.

Reading guide. Section 3 is the one-page answer: one row per gate with where it can run (HERE-PY, HERE-NODE, HERE-SWGL or GPU, defined in section 0), what already checks part of it and what stops a full run. Section 4 is the detail, one section per gate: the gate's words, what code and which test titles already exercise part of it (`Already checked`), which inputs exist in the repository (`Inputs present`), what is missing (`Missing`), the later additions in time order (`Added at the re-check`, `Updated at pass 4`, `Updated at pass 5`) and the `Runs` line. Section 2 describes the governance machinery, the facts in the code that bind any runner (2.4) and the history layer's data contract (2.5); section 5 the thresholds in code (5.1), the places where the code reads a gate more strictly than its tests (5.2), the owner-chat rows without gates (5.3) and what a runner must not do (5.4); section 6 the end-of-mission acceptance; sections 7 and 8 what was run and how the statements were checked. A statement older than the latest `Updated at pass N` bullet of its gate is superseded by it. Section 3.0 (sixth pass) is the table of the gate runners that now exist, 3.1 lists what moved at the sixth pass (3.1.1 what moved at the fifth), 3.2 the entry points a runner can call with their measured cost (3.2.1 the runners) and 3.3 the calls that were tried; section 2.6 describes the runners, their tests and what does not exist yet.

Gate wording is quoted from `GOVERNANCE/gates.json` (the same words as the plan text, Part E, Section Q, page 136 of `BIBLE/plan-clean.txt`).

## 0. How to read the "runs" line of each gate

| Mark | Meaning |
|---|---|
| HERE-PY | python 3.13.18 in this container (numpy 2.5.3, Pillow 12.3.0, shapely 2.2.0, pyproj 3.8.0 and OpenCV 5.0.0 present; scipy, astropy, skyfield, ephem absent; an astropy environment exists as scratch in `tmp/bt-gates/venv-astropy`) |
| HERE-NODE | node v22.23.4 in this container, no browser |
| HERE-SWGL | a browser in this container through `tools/horde/bench.sh`: Chrome for Testing 153.0.8010.12, software rendering only |
| GPU | needs the real GPU of `GOVERNANCE/hardware_profile.json` (NVIDIA RTX PRO 1000 laptop GPU). NOT RUNNABLE HERE |

GATE-SPEC-A.md (its U-10) uses the same four renderer classes under the names `python`, `node`, `swgl` and `gpu`; the marks here are the same classes.

A gate can have a state part (HERE-PY or HERE-NODE) and a picture part (HERE-SWGL). "Cannot pass on inputs" means the check can be executed here but an input the gate names does not exist yet, so every item it would judge is not passing.

### 1.1 The browser harness (`app/tests/lib.mjs`; the working-tree version of the first writing was committed at 19:11 UTC)

`app/tests/lib.mjs` in the working tree has environment switches that a gate runner calling the specs has to know: `BT_GL=auto|gpu|software` (auto tries the hardware flags and falls back to SwiftShader), `BT_API=webgpu|webgl2|auto`, `BT_DIST=<folder>` (serve a packed dist instead of the source tree), `BT_OUT=<name>` (numbers and pictures go to `app/tests/results/<name>/`; on a software renderer the default is `results/sw-latest/`), `BT_EXTRA=a=b&c=d` (more address parameters for every page a spec opens), `BT_WAIT_K` (debugging only), and, new since the first writing, `BT_SNAPSHOT=<folder>` (serve a frozen `git archive` of one commit instead of the live tree, because a software run takes hours and other workstreams keep editing), `BT_CYCLES` (the cycle count of `leak.spec.mjs`, 20 when unset, and the number in its test title) and the exported `REGISTER_ENTRY_STATUS` (200 from the source shell, 404 from a packed dist, where the register entries are withheld by design). Its `test` wrapper adds `t.gpuOnly(label, fn)` and `t.notRun(reason)`: on a software renderer the wrapped assertion is not evaluated and the run prints `# NOT RUN (NEEDS_GPU): <label>`. So a spec can end green here with parts not run: a gate runner must read those lines and count such a test as not run for the part it names, never as passed. The owner of the specs runs them one after another through `tmp/bt-spec/runq.sh` and logs each run under `app/tests/results/sw-round-1/` (`runs.tsv` and one `.log` per spec); those logs are that engineer's, read here and not reproduced. By 23:50 UTC the specs' owner had logged more runs on this container's software renderer (`app/tests/results/sw-round-1/runs.tsv` and one `.log` per run; `app/tests/results/SW-STATUS.md` is their summary, assembled by `results/sw-status.mjs` from the logs and the owner's hand verdicts in `verdicts.json`; read here and not reproduced, and every statement of this paragraph is the owner's). `webgl2.spec.mjs` on the live tree at 19:13 UTC on HEAD 1a8dd154 ended `not ok` after 352 s on the console line "Failed to load resource: the server responded with a status of 404 (Not Found)" (the shell's HEAD request for `src/ext/index.mjs`, a file created 26 s into that run; the owner classes it as neither a product nor a harness defect), then passed on a frozen copy of the source tree (`BT_SNAPSHOT`, a `git archive` of commit 9e1e1777; 463 s, started 20:55) and on a packed staging dist (`BT_DIST`; 543 s, started 21:31). `sky.spec.mjs` on the live tree passed 5 of 5 (started 19:19, 2,977 s). `real.spec.mjs` on the frozen copy passed 7 of 8 (started 21:03, 1,709 s) and the owner classed the one failure, the test that begins 'a placed unit', as a STALE_TEST (it read the card one minute after the end of the first of six paths, and a second march starts in that minute; the test now reads after the last path); its re-run (started 22:34) printed five passing tests and was cut off by the restart. Single-test diagnostic runs on the frozen copy: the test of a unit that has not arrived (`arrival.spec.mjs`) passed (935 s); the WebGL2 leak test ran with `BT_CYCLES=3`, so the cycle count in its title was 3 and not the 20 of the spec as written, and passed (538 s); the WebGL2 test of `casualties.spec.mjs` FAILED on the assertion 'the men stand up again: the renderer tables are rewritten, not left lying' (log line: back at 14:59 the picture differs from the first 14:59 picture by 48.951 %; the owner's cause was not known at 23:40); the paced WebGL2 frame-time test of `perf.spec.mjs` ended SKIPPED with five NEEDS_GPU assertions not run (620 s; the frame times it printed are 8.0 to 10.0 s at 1920 x 1080 with 165,000 placeholder soldiers and 360 guns: a software figure and not a timing of the product). In those logs a frame cost 4.5 to 8.5 s and the entry of the theatre 31 to 66 s (the wait scale K was 45 to 85), because the machine was loaded; the first logs (19:13 to 19:27) had given 3.9 s (webgl2.spec) and 5.2 s (sky.spec) per frame, 5.5 s p50 at 165,000 soldiers and 1280 x 720, entry 18 to 26 s and K of 39 to 52. `scrub.spec.mjs` was started at 23:37 (its log held only its header when read; the restart killed it and the log has no end line at 00:1x); `ui`, `structures3d`, the WebGPU tests and the other tests of `arrival`, `casualties`, `leak` and `perf` had no result yet. `SW-STATUS.md` (written 23:49) sums its owner's logs in one line, quoted as it stands: "69 tests in 10 specs: 1 NEEDS_GPU, 51 NOT, 15 PASS, 1 STALE_TEST, 1 UNCLASSIFIED" (the verdict column of its tables reads NOT RUN YET for a test that no run has reached). The owner's queue file (`tmp/bt-spec/queue2.txt`, read at 00:4x) lists the next five specs with time budgets of 3 to 12 hours each (`real` 10,800 s, `ui` 28,800, `perf` 21,600, `leak` 28,800, `structures3d` 43,200) and its queue runner holds one slot of the machine lock for hours at a time, so no further browser-spec result can be expected soon; a gate runner that needs a software-GL browser result has to budget hours, not minutes, and the second slot is shared with every other workstream. No browser spec was run for this note.

The specs' owner's work at the fifth pass (read at 23:19 UTC on 15 November and not reproduced; every statement is that owner's log): `app/tests/results/sw-round-1/runs.tsv` has no row newer than the one started at 22:34 UTC on 13 November, and `SW-STATUS.md` was regenerated at 23:35 on 15 November and keeps its summary line ("69 tests in 10 specs: 1 NEEDS_GPU, 51 NOT, 15 PASS, 1 STALE_TEST, 1 UNCLASSIFIED"): it now records the cut of `src-2-real` (5 passed, 0 failed of 5 finished; the test that begins "a placed unit", classed STALE_TEST, is marked fixed and passing in that re-run) and of `src-1-scrub` (nothing finished). Three logs gained a line or were created after the fourth pass. `src-1-scrub.log` and `src-2-real.log` end with the owner's line "CUT by the container restart of 2026-11-14 about 00:05Z (not a time limit, not a test result): the run did not finish; only the results above had been written" (the scrub run had printed its header and the slow-renderer line only, a 7,390 ms frame and an entry of 66 s; the real run had printed five passing tests). `diag-2-casualties-return.log` (a diagnostic of the casualties picture test on a frozen copy of commit c2704ba8, started 14 November 01:36:16 UTC) holds its header, a 4,135 ms frame, an entry of 35 s and the flags at entry, and no end line: the weekly limit ended the lane at about 01:39. The queue file `tmp/bt-spec/queue2.txt` as restarted at 22:57 UTC on 15 November lists `ui` (28,800 s), `perf` (21,600), `real` (10,800), `leak` (28,800) and `structures3d` (43,200), run by `tmp/bt-spec/runq.sh`, which was waiting for a slot of the machine lock at 23:19. A gate runner that needs a software-GL browser result of one of the specs therefore budgets hours, and `scrub`, `arrival` and `casualties` are not queued. No browser spec was run for this note.

The specs' owner's work at the sixth pass (read at 02:3x to 02:5x UTC on 16 November and not reproduced; every statement is that owner's record): `app/tests/lib.mjs` gained the field `SLOW.stepMs` (the most the page's clock advances in one frame, 100 ms) so that a loop inside the page that must cover a span of the page's clock accumulates `min(frame time, SLOW.stepMs)` per frame, as `wait()` does; `scrub.spec.mjs` (its pop test) and `casualties.spec.mjs` (its return visit) were edited at 00:50 UTC for the reasons given under Q12, with the owner's verdicts HARNESS and STALE_TEST ("fixed; browser re-run pending"); `runs.tsv` has one new row (`diag-2-casualties-return`, 00:32:02 to 01:12:14 UTC, 2,412 s, exit 1) and `SW-STATUS.md` (committed 01:21) says "69 tests in 10 specs: 1 HARNESS, 1 NEEDS_GPU, 50 NOT, 15 PASS, 2 STALE_TEST" (at the fifth pass: 1 NEEDS_GPU, 51 NOT, 15 PASS, 1 STALE_TEST, 1 UNCLASSIFIED); four new node reproductions of browser tests (`sw-round-1/pre/arrival-window-node.mjs`, `fx-camera-at-spawn.mjs`, `scrub-node.mjs` and `structures-node.mjs`, each with its log) run the logic of a spec on the real layer without a browser in 2 to 11 s; and `sw-round-1/audit/assertion-audit-44857c38.log` (02:36 UTC) compares every `assert.*` call and every `t.gpuOnly` wrapper of the ten specs at commit 44857c38 with the working tree as multisets, so that a weakened assertion would show (it lists, for example, the 7 assertions removed from and the 13 added to `arrival.spec.mjs` with the delegated conflict defaults, and the 18 added to `leak.spec.mjs`). No spec has a new browser result and none can be met by software rendering where it names a frame time or a hardware picture.

### 2.1 Files

| File | What it holds today |
|---|---|

### 2.3 The existing checkers of this workstream

- `tools/bt/check_governance.py` (exit 1 on a hard failure, writes `GOVERNANCE/check_report.json`): requirement ids exactly R01..R22; wording equal to `_req_src.json`; every requirement has sub-requirements; gate keys exactly Q01..Q28; every `gate_ids` entry known; `passes: true` is refused on a row with `source_status: seeded_unverified` and `origin: pdf`; every register entry has the eleven fields `title, rids, cat, desc, why, searched, subst, recon, tier, marking, status`, a tier from the three, and its file; no dangling register id; timeline rows: exactly 691, each with a tier, a documented row's `text_raw` does not end in an empty source cell (`| -` or `|`; that is the whole of the code's test for a source key), every seed row still `seeded_unverified`; conflict register exactly 47; no line of `decision_log.md` holds the word DECIDED without "DECIDED BY OWNER"; the decision log holds the string `DEC-` plus each conflict id (`DEC-C-001` to `DEC-C-047`). Links and coverage are reported as notes, never as failures.
- `tools/bt/check_coverage.py` (`coverage()`, exit 1 while any of the three fails): Q17 over `timeline_seed.jsonl` rows through the R11 rows and their open register ids, with `visual = set()` hard-wired (no realization file is read); Q18: requirements without sub-requirements, and rows neither passing nor linked to an open entry; Q23: register entries whose tier is not documented and whose `marking` is empty, "-", "none" or the "not stated by the shard proposal" marker, plus timeline rows without a tier. The realization workstream's test `tests/governance/realize-inputs.test.mjs` runs this script and compares its Q17 block with the harness (see Q17), so the JSON on stdout, the empty stderr and the meaning of `Q17.covered_by_open_register_entry` and `Q17.uncovered` are a contract with that workstream.
- `tools/bt/link_subreqs.py`: adds register ids to `register_ids` from `subreq_links.json` and `timeline_verified.jsonl` through `register_index.json` (`proposed_id`) and `register_aliases.json`; only open entries; never writes `passes`; re-serialises the whole file with its own indent.
- `tools/bt/register_from_proposed.py`: turns `GOVERNANCE/register/proposed/**/*.md` into entries (new ids continue EG-NNNN; updates become aliases; tier is the weakest named). It does not read `GOVERNANCE/horde/<workstream>/proposed-register/`. ### 2.4 Facts in this code that bind any runner or pass rule

1. `passes: true` on a PDF row needs `source_status` to leave `seeded_unverified` first, or `check_governance.py` fails. Only one value of `source_status` exists today. The vocabulary that exists elsewhere: `timeline_verified.jsonl status_effective` (seven values, 2.1) and `subreq_links.json status` (four values).
2. `check_governance.py` hard-codes 691 timeline rows, 47 conflicts and 28 gates. Adding the Gettysburg Address conflict that Q28 expects to `conflict_register.json` would fail the "conflict register != 47" check unless the checker is changed with it (see Q28).
3. The gate text of Q18 says 21 requirement IDs; the file and the checker have 22 (R22), plus the twelve owner-chat rows under `requirement_id: OWNER` (nine at the first writing).
4. The 14 epilogue rows carry Q17 but the Q17 scan reads `timeline_seed.jsonl` only.
5. Every R14.S row carries both Q07 and Q09 whatever its object is. Counted by the ids of the objects each of the 88 schedule rows names (`bld-` and `H-` as building or hospital, `FEN-` and `P-` as fence or wall, `E-` as work, `G-` as gun emplacement, `WS-` as epilogue state): 39 rows name only buildings or hospitals, 12 only works, 9 only epilogue states (S-080..S-088), 8 only fences or walls, 6 only gun emplacements, 4 a building and a fence, 2 a gun emplacement and a work, 1 a fence and a work, 3 only `ENV-` objects, 1 a fence and an `ENV-` object, 1 a `GROUP-` object, 1 a building and a `PERSON-` object, 1 a building and an `RD-` object; 2 rows are marked `out_of_battle_scope`. A per-item result needs an applicability rule by object kind; `check_structures.py` already splits this way (its Q07 loop takes records whose id starts FEN- or whose kind is stone_wall and schedule rows naming FEN- or P-01..P-14; its Q09 loop takes schedule rows naming `bld-` objects).
6. Scripts that write outside this workstream's ownership when run as a command (the third pass adds `tools/bt/kit_verified.py build`, which without `--out-dir` writes `kit/verified_oob.json` and `kit/verified_batteries.json`; `tools/bt/realize/check.mjs`, which without `--out` rewrites `GOVERNANCE/realizations/RESULTS.json`; `tools/bt/kit_overrides.py compile`, which writes `kit/overrides/<group>.json`; and `tools/bt/vegetation/build_vegetation.py` without `--check`): `tools/bt/check_environment.py` (its `main()` rewrites `environment/check_environment_results.json`), `tools/bt/check_figures.py --write` (`app/data/figures-validation.json`), every browser spec that writes `app/tests/results-*.json` or `shots-*`, `app/tests/run-all.mjs` (`results-summary.json`). A gate runner should import the gate functions (`check_environment.gate_q11`, `gate_q22`, `night_luminance_floor`; `check_structures.state_at`, `check_q07`, `check_q08`, `check_q09`, `check_scrub` with its `Gate` class; `check_arrivals.presence_ok`, `gate_time`; `check_kit.manifest_for`, `validate_manifest`) or run the command without the writing flag. The fourth pass adds the photo-match scripts: `tools/bt/photomatch/build_registry.py`, `build_statements.py`, `catalogue_additions.py` and `make_doc.py` write their own data files (and `app/docs/PHOTOMATCH.md`, `GOVERNANCE/evidence_corrections/web-photomatch.jsonl`); `wide_search.py` and `fetch_loc.py` read the open Library of Congress endpoints at one request per second; `run.mjs` renders in a browser (`tools/horde/bench.sh node tools/bt/photomatch/run.mjs [pose ...]`) and writes `resolved_poses.json` and `tmp/photomatch/renders`. None of them was run for this note. The fifth pass adds: `tools/bt/realize/buildlist.mjs --write` writes `GOVERNANCE/realizations/<shard>.MISSING.md`; `tools/bt/verify_staging.mjs --pack` packs into `deliver/` and a build that passes also refreshes `deliver/bt-staging/dist` and writes `deliver/bt-staging/LAST-VERIFY.txt`; `tools/bt/medical/validate_evidence.py --json PATH` writes the PATH it is given; `tools/bt/vegetation/gen_or27_more.py` and `gen_bachelder_claims.py` generate claim files; `tools/bt/photomatch/record_determinism.py`, `measure_p29_sheet.py` and `tie_geometry.py` write their own data files. None of them was run for this note.
7. The node tests of the app sample scenarios: `history.test.mjs` uses seven (`SCENARIOS`: the default and six choices over C-003, C-004, C-005, C-021, C-023, C-043). The layer names the conflicts that move gates in `layer.meta.linkedConflicts`. "Every scenario" in Q04 is larger than what any existing test runs.
8. Four browser specs run on the placeholder layer (`?history=stub`: `ui`, `sky`, `webgl2`, `perf`); five on the real layer (`leak`, `scrub`, `arrival`, `real`, `casualties.spec`) and `structures3d.spec` on the real layer. A result on the placeholder crowd says nothing about the history the product shows.
9. The browser specs and their harness were being changed by their owner while this was first written; the changed versions were committed in the 19:11 UTC checkpoint and `app/tests` had no uncommitted change to a spec, test or harness file at the re-check (git status, 19:2x UTC: the only modified file there was a run log under `results/sw-round-1/`). The committed `perf.spec.mjs` measures WebGL2 and WebGPU by name (`APIS = ['webgl2', 'webgpu']`), asserts `info.api === api` (the renderer asked for is the renderer measured) and wraps every threshold in `t.gpuOnly`; the earlier version, which asserted `info.api === 'webgpu'` although the shell default has been WebGL2 since DD-27 (`app/src/config.mjs chooseApi`), is gone. Titles quoted in section 4 are those of the files as they are now (`spec-b-evidence/verify_quotes.py` found 342 of the 344 quoted strings of this file verbatim at its last run and the other two, which contain an escaped inner quote, by hand). The frame-time and interaction tests of `perf.spec.mjs` and the first test of `leak.spec.mjs` run once per renderer, so those files hold more test instances than top-level `test(` calls.
10. A node runner can build the real layer exactly as the tests do: `loadRawFromDisk()` and `terrainMeta()` from `app/tools/history-report.mjs`, `createRealHistory({ raw, T, helpers })` and `SPEED` from `app/src/history/real.mjs`, `nodeWorld()` from `app/tests/node-world.mjs`, `worldFromUnits`, `Stage`, `scrubTest` (`sim/cache.mjs`). `layer.both(scenario)` returns `{ arrivals, events }`; `layer.report(scenario)` returns `offsets`, `state_offsets`, `defects`, `day_stops`, `casualties`.
11. Checkers a gate runner can call that live outside `tools/bt` (read at the re-check, not run unless section 7 says so): `docs/battle-theatre/history/undrawn/tools/check_undrawn.py` (validates the group files of the undrawn-units workstream: record shape, that ids resolve, that every quote of at most 14 words stands verbatim in the named local text at the given offset; exit 0 no error, 1 errors, 2 a source text is not on the machine, in which case the quote checks that need it are listed and not passed) and `derive_status.mjs` beside it (reads the history layer and writes `history/undrawn/status.json`: the drawn or not-drawn status of all 726 roster units); `docs/battle-theatre/fidelity/tools/check_quotes.py` (waypoint quotes of the fidelity pilot against the local texts of `fidelity/pilot/sources.json`); `tests/photomatch.test.mjs`; `tests/environment/*.test.mjs`; `tests/bt-pack/pack.test.mjs`; `docs/battle-theatre/sim/test/sim.test.mjs`; the check functions of `app/docs/GATE-HOOKS.md`; and, when they exist, the checks of the realization harness.
12. The stored `GOVERNANCE/realizations/RESULTS.json` is a record of an earlier state of the inputs: its `inputs_digest` is c3bccd4ac8fdb34e and `node tools/bt/realize/check.mjs --digests` gives 1c5066a209ff3320 at 23:00 on 15 November (the governance part 11 files against 12, the app source part 178 against 179). The harness's README and its build list call a result valid only while the digests equal the current ones, and the request note `bt-gates-realizations-q17-rules.md` says the gate runner runs `check.mjs --out <its own file>`: a runner does the same and never reads the stored file as the current state (a scratch run at 23:00 reproduced the stored `q17` block field for field).
13. Files that other lanes rewrite while a gate runs (the working tree at 23:0x UTC on 15 November: the vegetation data and tests, `kit/evidence/us-*.jsonl`, the photo-match registry and verdicts, the library pointers, the three `ext` modules, `GATE-HOOKS.md`, the realization tests) make a number printed from them true of its minute only. A runner records the sha256 of every input in its result file (the task's rule) and does not compare results of different digests; a test that counts a data file while its owner extends it can be red and green within the hour (5.4).
14. A runner writes `Qnn.json`, `Qnn.prev.json` and `evidence/Qnn/` under `--out` (default `GOVERNANCE/gate_results`): a run for an experiment that omits `--out` replaces the stored record and the `.prev.json` before it. Every runner takes `--no-write`, which computes and prints the verdict and writes nothing.
15. The entry points that GATE-SPEC 1.6 names (`run_gate.py`, `run_all.py`, `apply_passes.py`, `milestone.py`) and the files it says runners read or write (`gate_results/thresholds.json`, `SUMMARY.md`) do not exist (2.6); the only way to run a gate today is its own script, and no code reads a threshold registry.
16. `q19_claims.py` and `q23_marking.py` do not run under `python3 -I` (they import `q02_provenance` from a folder they do not put on `sys.path`); the other nine runners do. A runner started from a wrapper that uses `-I` for untrusted-data safety has to add `tools/bt/gates` to `PYTHONPATH` or be called as a script without it.
17. The items of Q02, Q19 and Q23 carry no `sub_requirements`, those of Q01, Q14 and Q20 do, and 45 of the 253 items of Q25 do: a step that maps item results to rows must derive the mapping by gate and class for the first three.
18. Two checkers of one property can disagree: Q23.7 (a non-documented register entry has a marking) is 10 of 385 by `check_coverage.py` and 11 of 385 by `q23_marking.py`, because `n/a` is an empty marking to the runner and not to the older script (EG-0006).
19. Scenario sets: Q01 and Q14 run by default over 7 scenarios (the default and the six of `history.test.mjs`: a sample, never S*); Q04, Q05 and Q06 are built over S* (333 scenarios in 10 interaction groups by the rule of `ArrivalModel.effective`, with the factorisation checked); any run on less, on a coarse time grid or with a class switched off is INCOMPLETE by construction (U-26), whatever its items say.

### 2.5 The history layer's data contract, read for the gates

Source: `app/data-contract.md` (version 1.0 with additions 1.1 to 1.8) and `app/README.md`; the real layer is `app/src/history/real.mjs` and the shapes are enforced by `app/src/history/contract.mjs` (`validateLayer`, `validateUnits`, `validateArrivals`, `validateEvents`, `validateKit`, `validateProvenance`, `validateDayPositions`, `validateCasualties`, `validateGuns`). A runner that judges units, arrivals, events and kits calls the layer (2.4 item 10), not the files behind it, so these are the facts of the contract it has to know. Nothing here is a pass rule.

| Contract item | What it fixes | Gates |
|---|---|---|
| Conventions | time is minutes since 1863-06-01 00:00 local mean time, the unit of `timeline_windows.jsonl` (`default`, `earliest`, `latest`); places are WGS84 and every position carries `sigma_m`; a tier is `documented`, `inferred`, `plausible-procedural` or `open gap`; "A tier of `documented` needs at least one source whose `kind` is not `lead`"; strengths and casualties are ranges with a declared basis (C-046, C-047) | Q02, Q05, Q12, Q17, Q19 |
| 1 `units` | `strength` `{min, max, basis, tier, sources}`, `formation`, `kit` id, `register` (an `EG-` id when the tier is not `documented`) | Q01, Q02, Q23 |
| 2 `arrivals(scenario)` | one arrival per unit, `earliest <= default <= latest`; `earliest` is the hard gate (Q04); the entry point lies off the loaded ground and the unit marches in; `conflict` names the C-0xx whose value gave the times | Q04, Q05, Q06 |
| 3 `events(scenario)` | `enter`, `path` (waypoints with `sigma_m`, linear between them, held after the last), `activity`, `state` (a man leaves the line, contract 1.3), `formation`, `leave`; the event cache refuses an `enter` before `earliest` and lists it | Q04, Q05, Q06, Q12, Q26 |
| 4 kit manifests | `kit(unitId, t)`; `validator.passed` is true, false or null (null: not run, with a note); "the validator, not the shell, decides" | Q03, Q14, Q20 |
| 5 `sources` | `kind` is `primary`, `secondary`, `lead`, `register` or `placeholder`, plus a `weak` flag | Q02, Q19 |
| 6 `objects` | a pre-existing feature is never given a construction animation (B.7.3); a built work does not exist before `built.from`; `objectProgress(obj, t)`; no work or wall of the record has a mapped line (a place and a ring of its sigma are drawn) | Q07, Q08 |
| Scenarios | `{ id, choices, optionText }`; empty `choices` is the contract's default, "PDF recommended, pending owner" (the shell's own default is the delegated one, `app/src/scenario-defaults.mjs`); a layer must honour every choice it declares (`meta.scenarioAware`), list the conflicts it uses in `meta.linkedConflicts`, and "must not silently pick among values" | Q04, Q12, Q19 |
| 1.1 | a strength may have `min: null` (a procedural share, never `documented`, with `drawn` figures instanced one to one); an arrival may have `entry: null` with an `entry_status` (an open gap, not a guess); `render.status` is `moving`, `edge_only`, `not_drawn`, `aggregate` or `not_on_field`; `report(scenario).defects`; `layer.counts` | Q01, Q04, Q06 |
| 1.2 | `unit.approach` (the nearest printed road of the unit's own line of command, its tier, its place in the column); `report().offsets` of kind `ledger_vs_edge` and `cascade` are "a data property, not a defect" (the gate is not moved and the march is not shortened); `leave` only for a printed departure | Q05, Q06 |
| 1.3 | `casualties` (ranges per fate; `state` events only inside the printed engagement windows, a missing man is counted and not animated); `guns` drawn only for a battery whose count the kit table states (no default count is ever applied); per-figure manifests and the python validation result `data/figures-validation.json`; formation changes | Q01, Q03, Q14, Q26 |
| 1.4 to 1.8 | a claim of the position ledgers is used only if `claim_support_verified` is true, `fidelity_check` is `passed_full_read` or `corrected` and its tier is `documented` or `inferred`; a placement made from a claim is `inferred`; `unit.provenance` with `validateProvenance`; `data/claim-dispositions.json` has one row for every claim read; day positions of the troop maps (a day, never a minute; the time is modelled; `state_offsets`); ledger E; a `documented_absent` unit is not drawn and has no arrival, figure or casualty share | Q02, Q04, Q06, Q19 |
| The structures layer | not part of the contract: `data/structures3d.json`, `structures/state_schedule.json` and `structures/hospitals.json`; its one link to the layer is `layer.objects` | Q07, Q08, Q09, Q26 |

What `layer.report(scenario)` returns for a scenario (contracts 1.2, 1.3, 1.5 and 1.7): `offsets`, `state_offsets`, `defects`, `day_stops`, `casualties`, `activity_notes`, `counts`. The contract names two things a pass rule has to take a position on: a late arrival that the layer reports and keeps (a data property, not a defect), and the default scenario (contract: empty choices; shell: the delegated defaults of `decisions/conflict_defaults.json`). At the fifth pass `app/data-contract.md` and `app/README.md` have no commit after 00:16 UTC on 14 November (018558fe), and `app/src/history/`, `app/src/stage.mjs`, `app/src/config.mjs` and `docs/battle-theatre/sim/` none after 82d95249: the layer and its contract are the ones this section describes. The README section "Units the layer does not draw" (added in that commit) holds the counts quoted in Q01 and Q06 (726 units of the roster; drawn placed 418 and at the edge of the ground 21; not drawn 109 (108 leaf units and the 32d Massachusetts, which the roster holds as a formation); formations 164; not on the field 14).

At the sixth pass `app/data-contract.md` and `app/README.md` still have no commit after 00:16 UTC on 14 November (018558fe), and `app/src/history/`, `app/src/stage.mjs`, `app/src/config.mjs` and `docs/battle-theatre/sim/` are the same (`pass6/inputs_delta.py`: none of the Q04 and Q05 inputs under them changed), so the contract above is the one the runners of Q01 to Q06 read. Two of its facts now appear as results: the 108 open-gap leaf units that the layer does not draw are 108 of the 133 Q01.5 failures and the failing part of the Q06.3 domain (the other 25 failures are formations above them), and a late arrival that the layer reports and keeps is read by Q05.4 as BLOCKED-INPUT until an open L3 register entry holds it (DD-G-Q05-04).

### 2.6 The gate runners of this workstream (`tools/bt/gates`, `tests/governance/gates`)

This section is new at the sixth pass: when the first five passes were written the folder `tools/bt/gates/` did not exist (2.1 says so in its lists of what was absent). It holds, at 02:3x UTC on 16 November, 34 tracked files (9,764 lines of python and node code, with the runners' data files) and `tests/governance/gates/` 36 tracked files (6,333 lines of python, 14 node mutation modules); both were first committed in the checkpoints of 15 November 23:5x to 16 November 00:5x and are in HEAD. They implement the executable definition of GATE-SPEC.md for 11 of the 28 gates and are the answer to the question of this file in its strongest form: for those 11 gates "what is missing to run it" is no longer a description but a result file.

The test files together hold 585 test methods in 14 files; the fixtures are `gates_fixtures.py`, `pq_fixtures.py`, `pq_world.py`, `q03_world.py` and `q456_fixtures.py`. A test of the first kind feeds a runner a synthetic dump of the layer or a synthetic table and asserts that each injected defect turns the right check red; a test of the second kind (`RealData`, `RealProduct`, `SceneChild`) runs the real node child on the real history layer, world and stage and writes into a scratch folder; a third kind injects a defect into the REAL code path with `--mutate FILE.mjs` (the module exports `mutateRaw`, `mutateLayer`, `mutateWorld`, `mutateStage` or `afterLoad`; 14 modules in `q456_mutations/` for Q04 to Q06) and asserts that the real child's verdict changes. The mutation checks (`mutation_check*.py`) copy `tools/bt/gates`, disable one check in the copy and require a unit test to go red: the stored text of Q01, Q14 and Q20 reads "caught 48 of 48" (00:39 UTC on 16 November); the mutation checks of Q03 and Q04 to Q06 exist and were not run at this pass.

**The record and the conventions, as implemented** (`lib/pq_common.py`, GATE-SPEC 1.6). Schema `bt-gate-result/1`; header keys `gate`, `run_id`, `started_utc`, `finished_utc`, `command`, `tool_versions`, `renderer_class` (`python`, `node` or `mixed` for the runners here; no runner has a browser or GPU part), `runnable_here`, `inputs` (path, bytes, role and sha256 of every file read, with `missing: true` for an input that is absent), `input_hashes` (the same as a map), `thresholds` (id, value, decision, metric), `scenario_set` and `time_grid` where they apply, `counts` and `totals` (per check and per result), `verdict` (PASS, FAIL or INCOMPLETE), `incomplete_reasons`, `owed`, `notes`, and `items` (id of the form `Qnn/Qnn.k/<subject>/<scenario>`, `check`, `pass`, `result`, `reason`, `evidence`, and where they apply `subjects`, `sub_requirements`, `scenario`). The result words are PASS, COVERED, FAIL, NOT-RUN, BLOCKED-INPUT and N/A; `pass` is true only for PASS; an item whose input is missing is BLOCKED-INPUT with a reason that begins "input missing:", never a pass; items, evidence lists and totals are sorted so that two runs on the same inputs give identical items and the clock lives only in the header. A runner writes `<out>/Qnn.json`, replaces the one before as `<out>/Qnn.prev.json` and puts sidecars in `<out>/evidence/Qnn/`; `--out DIR` redirects all three, `--no-write` prints only; it never writes a `passes` flag. Exit codes: 0 PASS, 1 FAIL, 2 INCOMPLETE, 3 runner error (nothing is written then, so a good earlier record is not clobbered). `sub_requirements` is on every item of Q01, Q14 and Q20 (Q01: R04.0000 and R10.0000; Q14: R03.0000, R04.0000 and the R13.G rows; Q20: R04.0000 and R16.0000), on 45 of the 253 items of Q25 (the 45 R12 rows), and on NO item of Q02, Q19 or Q23 (found at pass 6): a later step that sets `passes` per row has to map those items to rows by gate and class (for example the conflict items to the 47 `R03.C` rows), which no code does yet.

**What does not exist** (checked at 02:3x UTC): `run_gate.py`, `run_all.py`, `apply_passes.py` and `milestone.py` of GATE-SPEC 1.6 (the last stage of the workflow `bt-gate-engine.mjs` writes `run_all.py` and `apply_passes.py`), `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/thresholds.json` and `SUMMARY.md`, a runner for each of Q07 to Q13, Q15 to Q18, Q21, Q22, Q24 and Q26 to Q28 (17 gates), and a stored result for Q03 to Q06: the dense runs of Q04, Q05 and Q06 over S* (333 scenarios in 10 interaction groups) were queued behind the machine lock on 16 November at 01:24 and 01:47 (`tmp/bt-gates/q456/run_q04_full.sh`, `run_q05_full.sh`, `run_q06_full.sh`, from frozen copies of the runners under `snap1/` and `snap2/`) and their logs are empty: the owner's full pause at 01:53 UTC stopped every workflow and the leftover jobs (`horde/CAMPAIGN.md`), and the container restarted at about 02:20 UTC. No runner reads `thresholds.json` (it does not exist): each carries its constants and records them as `thresholds` in its own record (T-Q02-1 14 words, T-Q05-1 1.0 m, T-Q05-2 1.0001, T-Q06-1 90 s), so GATE-SPEC 1.7's rule that a threshold with no decision id makes a gate INCOMPLETE is not applied by any code.

**Findings about the runners at the sixth pass** (a specification pass changes no code; each is for the runners' owner, which is this workstream's later stages). (1) `q19_claims.py` and `q23_marking.py` insert only `tools/bt/gates/lib` on `sys.path` and import `q02_provenance` from the folder above it, so `python3 -I tools/bt/gates/q19_claims.py` fails with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'q02_provenance'`; started as a script they work, and the other nine runners insert their own folder and run under `-I`. (2) The same property, Q23.7 (a non-documented register entry has a marking), is counted differently by `check_coverage.py` (10 of 385) and by `q23_marking.py` (11 of 385): the first treats only `-`, `none` and the shard-proposal marker as an empty marking, the second also `n/a`, which EG-0006 carries. (3) The end-to-end tests read the working tree: they passed between 02:40 and 02:54 UTC and can go red when another workstream changes the code or data they exercise, so a red `RealData` test is read as in 5.4. (4) `test_q03.py` is heavy (117 tests, 490 s of wall time and 128 s of CPU at a load of 20 to 30, run alone); the other thirteen files need 0.07 to 39 s of CPU each (the Q04 file 34.6 s when run alone and 38.9 s in the light run). (5) The runners hash the data and the application code they read but not their own source: a record does not say which version of the runner made it (`lib/extracts.py`, which Q01, Q02 and Q14 import, was modified at 00:41:19 on 16 November, after the Q01 record finished at 00:36:33 and the Q14 record at 00:35:41). (6) A re-run into the default folder replaces the stored record and its `.prev.json`; experiments use `--out`.

**What a runner for one of the other 17 gates can reuse** (names read from the files at 02:5x UTC). `lib/pq_common.py`: `Env` (the root, `--out`, `p()` and `btp()` path helpers, so that a test can hand a runner a scratch tree with the same layout), `Record` (items, thresholds, owed, notes, counts, verdict, the atomic write of `Qnn.json`), `InputMissing`, `sha256_file`, `is_placeholder`, `run_node`, `base_parser`, `finish_cli` and `guarded_main` (the exit codes). `oobgates_common.py`: `layer_dump` (runs a node child that builds the real layer and returns its JSON dump, a failed child being exit 3 and never a pass), `gate_rows` and `sub_requirements_for` (the rows of `requirements.json` an item bears on, with the population rows `R10.U-<unit>` and `R17.U-<unit>` once they exist), `register_state` and `covered_by_register` (whether a register entry exists, is open, has its eleven fields and `owner_q` and a dated search log: the L3 test of GATE-SPEC 1.4). `lib/extracts.py`: `fold`, `numbers_in_span`, `quote_ok` and `TextStore` (the one function that finds a source extract again in the stored text, with `no_text` where the text is not on the machine). `lib/datum_scan.py` with `datum_classes.json` (55 datum classes read into normalised datums) and `lib/loadlist.py` (the page's load list). `q456_lib.mjs` (the real layer, world and stage, the scenario set S*, the time grid, the `--mutate` hook and the list of every file the run read). For the hooks that already exist as pure functions (`checkVegetation`, `checkScene`, `checkLaterMemorials`, `checkRun`, `checkCompare`, `selectFigures`, `state_at`, `gate_q11`, `gate_q22`) a runner is a thin wrapper that calls them and wraps each result in items; `app/docs/GATE-HOOKS.md` and 3.2 list them.

### 3.3 Calls that were tried at the fifth pass

Python, from the repository root with `python3 -I` and `sys.path.insert(0, 'tools/bt')`; read-only, each under 4 s (23:2x UTC on 15 November):

- `import check_structures as cs; G = cs.Gate('Q07'); cs.check_q07(G)` leaves `G.n` = 191 checks and `G.fails` empty; the same holds for `check_q08`, `check_q09`, `check_integrity` and `check_scrub`. A `Gate` counts checks and keeps failure messages: it has no result per schedule row, so a runner that needs one item per row builds it from `state_at` and the schedule rows, or reads `G.fails` as messages.
- `cs.state_at('FEN-emmitsburg-road', '1863-07-03T16:00', 'default')` returns a dict with the keys `structure_id`, `time`, `scenario`, `rows` (here `['FEN-emmitsburg-road:chg0', 'S-062']`) and `suppressed`; an id that is not in `structures/structures.json` raises `KeyError('unknown structure id: ...')`.
- `import check_arrivals as ca; ca.gate_time('us-1c', 'default')` returns `('1863-07-01T10:00', 'us-1c')`: the gate time and the unit whose ledger row gives it; `ca.presence_ok('us-1c', '1863-07-01T08:00', 'default')` returns `False` (the unit is not yet there). Unit ids are those of `history/units.json`.
- `import check_coverage as cc; cc.coverage()` returns a dict with the keys `Q17`, `Q18` and `Q23` and the numbers of 2.3.

Node: the two vegetation checks are called exactly as `spec-b-evidence/pass3/veg_check.mjs` (`new VegModel(data)` over `app/data/vegetation.json`, then `checkVegetation(M)`) and `pass4/veg_scene_check.mjs` (`checkScene(M, { project, ground, trees, decals })`, with the terrain from `nodeWorld()` of `app/tests/node-world.mjs` and `buildTrees` and `buildDecals` of `app/src/ext/vegetation/mesh.mjs`) do; both ran at 23:00 UTC and print the figures of Q21. The realization harness is run as `node tools/bt/realize/check.mjs --out <file>` and read from that file. Every other hook is in `app/docs/GATE-HOOKS.md` (21,732 bytes at 23:10 UTC; its newest section, for Q24, was uncommitted), under the gate it serves in section 4.

### Q01

> Unit counts and strengths match the verified OOB table.

- **Already checked.** `python tools/bt/check_arrivals.py` validates `history/units.json` and `arrivals.json` and runs self-tests (today: "units: 726  rows: 44  subunit gates: 9  gates: 56  unparsed cells: 4", "check_arrivals: OK"). `history/crosscheck.json` compares the regiment lists printed in the ledger rows with the B.3 lists. app/tests/history.test.mjs: "contract: the real layer validates (units, arrivals, events, kit) in every scenario; 726 units, ids unique"; "strengths: printed figures are single figures with no invented range; shares are procedural, weak, never documented, and add up to their pool"; "world from units: one world unit per layer unit, one soldier per figure (no cap), undrawn units have none"; "what is drawn: only units with a printed road of approach; every drawn unit has an entry, every other leaf says why not; counts add up". ledgerE.test.mjs: "the 84th Pennsylvania: documented absent, not drawn, no arrival, no figure, not counted in the field totals; ...". anim3d-onetoone.test.mjs: "one-to-one: nothing in src/anim3d or src/figures3d caps the figure count (no MAX_FIGURES, no figure budget constants); the cap would be an owner decision". casualties.test.mjs: "shares: the printed figures are kept, every pool is shared exactly, no share exceeds the strength share, the unit validators accept them". All of these compare the layer with its own source (the plan's B.2.4 and B.3 tables); none compares it with a verified table.

Browser: arrival.spec.mjs "across the whole window, in every frame sampled: nothing is drawn before its window opens, and the renderer was told exactly what the stage holds", "an event before a unit's earliest arrival is refused by the sim event cache (Q04), listed, and the page says so", "a unit that has not arrived is not drawn (pixels), is absent from the unit list and says so on its card; then it marches in from the edge and is drawn"; webgl2.spec.mjs "WebGL2 renders the placeholder crowd, hides units before their arrival, answers the scrubber, and leaves no context behind" (stub).

The child drives the REAL product (history layer, world, the `Stage` the page draws, `ArtilleryTrain` as `Structures.drawLists` runs it, the per-figure manifests, the crowd animation driver and the `AnimSystem` that makes every shot, smoke puff, march sound and fall) over the scenario set S* and the time grid of GATE-SPEC 1.5 and reports, per unit and entity class, the maximal runs of grid times at which the class is present; python judges them against bounds that it reads itself from `history/arrivals.json` through the rule of `check_arrivals.gate_time`: Q04.1 absolute (presence zero for every class of every unit in every scenario before the minimum over all documented arrival values), Q04.2 scenario-local (before the window start the scenario selects; no layer event and no event refused by the sim cache before it), Q04.3 entry (first presence at the edge of the loaded ground, in column of march), Q04.4 departure (a march out; no entity vanishes), Q04.5 unknown arrivals (tier below documented, an open register entry, no earlier than the floor event), Q04.6 the named cases (V Corps, II Corps, Longstreet's divisions, Stuart's brigades, Pickett, the VI Corps, units never or partly on the field), Q04.7 effects (no shot, smoke, march sound, command or fall, and no casualty on the ground, before the unit's window opens). S* is built by DD-G-Q04-01: the Cartesian product inside each interaction group of the 12 conflicts that reach an arrival or a leg (333 scenarios in 10 groups today, the full product being about 10^10), with every other conflict at its delegated default, plus the two named defaults, the earliest and latest extremal scenarios and "show every value"; the factorisation is not taken on trust (the per-unit behaviour of every scenario is compared with the default and a unit that changes without depending on a varied conflict is reported). An item is (unit, behaviour of the unit under S*), naming every scenario it stands for (DD-G-Q04-03); a run on the coarse grid, on a part of S* or with a class switched off is INCOMPLETE, never PASS; Q04 has no COVERED branch. ### Q05

> Position-time plausibility: speeds on foot, horse, limber, wagon and in column within documented ranges; no teleport; continuous trajectories.

- **Already checked.** app/tests/history.test.mjs "events: time ordered, no leave, every enter is off the loaded ground and at the unit's path start, no movement faster than double-quick, arrival is a march", "positions are continuous and no faster than double-quick: every path begins where the last one ended (or where the unit entered), within a metre, and a unit is never between two places without a path"; daypos.test.mjs "speeds: nothing moves faster than double-quick, and a leg to a day position is a march at the documented rate"; unit.test.mjs "no pops over the whole window: in 1-minute steps every unit moves at most marching speed; units enter and leave only off the loaded ground" (stub); structures-ws6.test.mjs "Q05: the train moves only with its battery: no entity jumps (displacement between two stage frames is bounded) and it keeps its place relative to the battery (heading eased)"; crowd.test.mjs "block turn time scales with the formation extent (a rigid train turned in 25 s moved its far end at 30 m/s)"; anim3d.test.mjs "drill tables: the marching rates are those of Hardee and Casey (90 x 28 in. common time, 110 quick time and route step, 165 x 33 in. double quick, 180 urgent)"; anim3d-gaits.test.mjs (14 tests on the printed horse paces of Wheeler 1863, Cooper 1861 and the Instruction for Field Artillery 1863). Browser: scrub.spec.mjs "no unit pops while playing at 60x: every unit moves at most double-quick speed per frame; units enter and leave the field only off the loaded ground". The bound in code is `SPEED = { march: 1.25, double_quick: 4000 * 0.9144 / (25 * 60) }` m/s in `app/src/history/real.mjs`, asserted as `v <= SPEED.double_quick * 1.0001 + 1e-9`.
- **Inputs present.** The layer's `path` events; `app/data/anim3d/gait-sources.json`, `drill-timing.json`; `drill/*.json`.
- **Missing.** Ranges by mover: the layer moves every unit, mounted or not, at the two foot rates, and the tests bound every mover with the foot double-quick. ### Q10

> Night visibility check. At every night time, render test frames and assert minimum luminance and contrast for every unit and activity in view; the sky is dimmed; moon and stars follow the ephemeris.

- **Already checked.** Browser, app/tests/sky.spec.mjs (stub layer, 1280 x 720, one camera 55 m from one placeholder unit): "night frames keep luminance and contrast with units in view (Q10), and are dimmer than the day" at ten night times with the assertions written in the spec: mean luminance at least 24 of 255, 5th percentile at least 6, fewer than 2 % of pixels below 8, Michelson contrast (p95 - p5)/(p95 + p5) at least 0.2, night mean below 0.75 of the day mean, sun below -6 degrees; "the luminance floor is a parameter: raising it brightens the night monotonically, and its lowest setting still leaves the night visible"; "the light follows the table: at night the light comes from the moon where the sky table puts it; by day from the sun"; "the day and night bar under the picture shows the same sky: ...". Node: unit.test.mjs "night is dimmed but never black: exposure never falls below the floor, and falls with the sun"; weather3d.test.mjs "Q10: sky, fog and cloud radiance never fall below the floor at any minute of the battle, in every scenario of the conflicts", "stars: only at night, never in the day, hidden by cloud, mist and a bright moon; the sky stays lit when they show", "luminance floor in the shader: the sky is clamped to the floor uniform, lights only add (multiply >= 1), nothing subtracts below zero on geometry except the exact fog undo", "sky geometry: sun and moon directions come from the project ephemeris; sun above the horizon by day, below at night"; fx3d.test.mjs "night is never black: smoke radiance and the effect ambient stay above the floor at every minute of the battle; flashes and fuse trails appear only when they should"; anim3d.test.mjs "state machine: night camp shows sleeping, fires and pickets; day camp shows sitting, cooking, eating (R18)". Python: `check_environment.night_luminance_floor(time_iso)` returns the parameters and renders nothing; it returns the sentence "OPEN: the PDF gives no numeric minimum luminance or contrast for gate Q10; to be set by the owner, not guessed".
- **Inputs present.** `environment/ephemeris_1863.json`, `weather_timeline.json` (`moonlight_by_night`, `night_light_parameters`), `app/src/config.mjs DEFAULTS.nightFloor` 0.34.
- **Missing.** Frames at every night time (the spec samples ten). Luminance and contrast for every unit and activity in view: per-unit screen regions on the real layer, including the night activities. Numeric thresholds with an owner or delegated decision behind them: the five numbers above are the spec's own, no entry of `decisions/DELEGATED-DECISIONS-2026-11-11.md` sets them, and Section Q says thresholds are set at gate creation from the vertical slice and changed only by owner decision. Stars and moon in rendered frames: the README says the prototype sky cannot show stars; the weather layer adds them on WebGL2 and its picture checks are listed as owed in `app/docs/BROWSER-VERIFICATION-LIST-WEATHER.md`.
- **Added at the re-check.** The specs' owner began logging software-GL runs to `app/tests/results/sw-round-1/src-1-sky.log` (read at 19:27 UTC; not mine, not reproduced). At 3 Jul 13:30 it printed mean luminance 168.0 of 255, p5 46, p95 214, contrast 0.65; at 3 Jul 20:30 (sun -10 degrees, moon down, exposure 0.38) mean 28.7, p5 17, p95 43, contrast 0.43, near-black pixels 0.3 %. Those are two of the spec's ten times, on the placeholder layer, from software rendering, against the spec's own numbers. A frame took 5.2 s in that run, so every 1,000 frames cost about 87 minutes: in this container a pixel scan of the night can only be a sample, and no file read sets the sampling rule.
- **Updated at pass 4.** The WebGPU path of the weather and effects layers (`app/src/weather3d/gpu.mjs`, `shaders-wgsl.mjs`) has its own harness (`app/tests/weather3d-gpu*.mjs`; `GATE-HOOKS.md` lists it as a source of numbers for Q10, Q22 and Q24) and `weather3d-gpu.test.mjs` (23 `test(` calls, 26 tests run, 26 pass at 00:15 UTC, 65 s of wall time and 2 s of CPU); its last test is "stored evidence: the WebGPU and WebGL2 runs are clean, the two APIs agree to the figures the notes state, the other budget tiers are clean, and the 16 pictures are there". The stored run (`app/tests/results/weather3d-webgpu/`, started 21:25 UTC on 13 November: 640 x 360, 12,000 placeholder soldiers, software adapters on both APIs) has 16 cases, eight of them at night or dusk by their names, and records per case the luminance statistics of the read-back picture in 8-bit display values: the means of those eight run from 34.0 (`stars-jun28-0200`) to 106.5 (`moon-zoom-jul3-2330`), the black share is 0 in 15 of the 16 cases and 0.00001 in `flash-night-jul2-2330` (minimum 0, about two pixels of 230,400), on both APIs (their whole-frame means differ by at most 0.02 levels on those eight cases, and the whole-frame minimum differs in two of them: `dusk-jul2-2000` 23.07 on WebGPU against 8 on WebGL2, `moon-zoom-jul3-2330` 52.95 against 53.16; the mean absolute difference per channel between the two pictures of a case is 0.33 to 0.46 levels over the 16 cases, `compare-summary.json`, against a harness limit of 6) (the summary line of section 3 said "black share 0 in each" before this pass: wrong by that one case). It measures sky, fog, weather and effects in one stand-in view per case, not the units and activities of the real layer, and none of the five numbers of `sky.spec.mjs` (mean at least 24 and the rest) is applied to it by any code (`checkRun` calls a picture blank only when its mean is not above 0). The stars, the moon and the night floor are now drawn on WebGPU as well as WebGL2 (the README's list of what is not done still says "the sky cannot show stars", which is true of the prototype's own sky; the weather layer adds them). The hook `checkRun(summary, { cases })` makes a run started with `--real-gpu` a problem when its recorded adapter is a software one. Nothing here ran on a real GPU.

### Q11

> Ephemeris check: sun and moon values equal an independent ephemeris at the site and times.

- **Already checked.** `tools/bt/ephemeris.py`: method A (the builtin ephemeris of astropy through ERFA) and method B (its own Meeus implementation; "nothing in B imports or calls astropy"), per-minute grid, events, lunar phases. `tools/bt/check_environment.py gate_q11(recompute)`: tolerances in the code are 0.05 degrees for the sun, 2.0 minutes for rise and set, 0.5 degrees for the moon (comment: "tolerances given by the task owner"), the stored table against a fresh method A within 0.0006 degrees, the full moon of 1 July within 1.0 minute; `pdf_figure_checks()` compares the plan's printed sun and moon figures with the table and records differences as findings about the plan. Stored: `environment/ephemeris_1863.json`, `ephemeris_agreement.json`, `check_environment_results.json`. Node: app/tests/unit.test.mjs "sky: the table is read where it exists; the formula for 7-14 Jul agrees with the table where both exist"; weather3d.test.mjs "sky geometry: ..."; browser sky.spec.mjs "the light follows the table: ..." (stub). The main site's own sky data has a separate validator: `tests/environment/astro.test.mjs` ("astro_by_day validates (schema, independent recomputation, USNO and NASA cross-checks)", "astro_by_tick validates: 675 ticks, every row recomputed independently", and its seeded-defect tests) and `tests/environment/verify-astro.test.mjs`. Re-run at 19:3x UTC (`spec-b-evidence/environment_tests.out`): `astro.test.mjs` 30 tests, 29 pass, 1 skipped; `units.test.mjs` 8 of 8 pass; `verify-astro.test.mjs` 3 of 6 fail, and its header says it is an adversarial verifier whose tests fail by design on the committed state of the main site's data and validator. These suites read `tools/environment/` and `data-src/environment/`, not the Battle Theatre's `environment/` files, so they are not inputs of gate Q11; `ephemeris.py` has `compare_with_astro_by_day` and `compare_with_astro_by_tick` against those files.
- **Inputs present.** All of the above; the table covers 26 June to 6 July and 18 to 19 November 1863.
- **Missing.** In this container: astropy. `python tools/bt/check_environment.py --no-recompute` exits 1 with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'astropy'` (the saved agreement is used for A against B, but `lunar_phases()` still calls method A). For 7 to 14 July the page uses the formula of `app/src/astro.mjs`, compared with the table where both exist; no independent ephemeris covers those days. The checker's own caveats: both lunar series descend from the same theory and both methods share one Delta T polynomial.
- **Observed.** With astropy 8.0.2 and pyerfa 2.0.1.5 installed from PyPI into a scratch environment (`tmp/bt-gates/venv-astropy`, not the system python) and the gate functions imported (not `main()`): `gate_q11(True)` 13 of 13 checks ok in 30.8 s (largest A minus B: sun altitude 0.0023 degrees, sun azimuth 0.0083, moon altitude 0.00016, moon azimuth 0.0006; rise and set at most 0.013 minutes; stored table against fresh A 0.0005 degrees); `gate_q11(False)` 12 of 12 ok; `pdf_figure_checks()` 134 printed figures: 119 MATCH, 3 MARGINAL, 1 MATCH(trunc), 6 FINDING, 5 INFO. The re-check ran the same functions again at 19:45 UTC (`spec-b-evidence/q11_q22_inmemory.py`, output `q11_q22_inmemory.out`, through the lock; no file under `environment/` changed) and got identical figures: 13 of 13 in 25.9 s, largest A minus B 0.0023 degrees (sun altitude), 0.0083 (sun azimuth), 0.00016 (moon altitude), 0.0006 (moon azimuth), 0.0144 minutes for all events; 12 of 12 with the saved agreement; 134 figures with the same status counts; Q22 31 of 31. Its six FINDING rows are differences between the plan's printed figures and the computation, kept as findings about the plan and not hidden: the moonrise of 30 June printed "about 19:10" against a computed 19:01:18 (-8.7 min); the moon's illumination at the end of the nights of 1, 2, 3 and 4 July printed 93, 86, 77 and 68 % against a computed 97.7, 92.3, 84.3 and 74.5 %; and the sunrise of 2 July in T2-052 printed "about 04:29 local" against a computed 04:35:15 (+6.25 min).

### Q12

> Scrub test. Random-access seeks (forward and backward, including jumps across the whole window) produce state identical to sequential playback at the same time; no unit pops; the cache header seed and code version match.

- **Already checked.** `sim/cache.mjs`: `EventCache` (snapshots, `header()` with `seed, code, scenario, inputHash, snapshotEvery, events, rejected`) and `scrubTest(cache, tMin, tMax, n, rnd)`; `sim/test/sim.test.mjs` "Q12 scrub: random seeks, backward and forward identical", "late insertion invalidates only later snapshots". app/tests (node): unit.test.mjs "Q12 scrub: random-access seeks give the state of sequential playback (cache level and live tables), forward and backward" (stub); history.test.mjs "purity: arrivals, events and the world are the same whatever the order of evaluation (shuffled scenarios, fresh layers, repeated calls)", "stage with the real layer: ... purity (Q12)"; casualties.test.mjs "the stage is pure in time: shuffled seeks give the same live tables, state hash and soldier tables as sequential playback, forward and back"; the purity tests of claims, daypos, sibling and ledgerE; structures3d.test.mjs "state_at is pure: ...", "state integration: ..."; structures-ws6.test.mjs "purity: the draw lists depend only on the time and the choices; scrubbing forwards, backwards and shuffled gives the same lists", "works purity: ..."; anim3d.test.mjs "determinism: clipAt is a pure function of (k, t): any query order gives the same answer", "determinism: the events of consecutive windows equal the events of one big window (seek = sequential play)"; anim3d-gaits.test.mjs "gaits: events are deterministic (two runs identical) and a seek returns the same hoofbeats as sequential play"; cavalry-ws10.test.mjs "scrub purity: ..."; crowd.test.mjs "the animation of every figure on the field is a pure function of time (order of evaluation does not matter)"; fx3d.test.mjs "spawn determinism: ...", "a burst or impact earlier than the shot that caused it is refused; a seek clears the store and asks for a replay window"; weather3d.test.mjs "timeline: deterministic, a pure function of t (scrub order does not matter)", "fx-host: wires fx3d to the anim bus (fire -> smoke and flash), replays after a seek ONLY into the effect handlers, and never emits an effect before its cause"; audio-host.test.mjs "causal clock: events after the clock are dropped, a seek back stops every voice and echo and empties the queue, nothing from the old future survives". Python: `check_structures.py` "Q12-analogue scrub consistency PASS (1441 checks, 0 failures)". Browser (real layer): scrub.spec.mjs "scrubber: random-access seeks equal sequential playback (Q12); the cache header carries seed and code version" (61 states ten minutes apart revisited in shuffled order with detours to both ends; asserts `header.seed === 1863`, `header.code === 'bt-sim-0.1'`, an 8-hex `inputHash`), "scrub across the full range from the keyboard: Home/End/PageUp/PageDown on the scrubber agree with the sequential state too", "pixel identity: after a detour, the picture at a time is the picture it was before (paused)", "no unit pops while playing at 60x: ..."; structures3d.spec.mjs "scrub purity and no leak: ..."; casualties.spec.mjs "${api}: men of a unit fall in the picture while it stands in a printed window, stand up again when the scrubber goes back, and the same time gives the same picture".

What "match" means for the header: `sim/cache.mjs CODE_VERSION` is `bt-sim-0.1`, `app/src/config.mjs` has `SEED = 1863` and a second string `SIM_CODE = 'bt-app-0.1'`; the spec compares the header with literals. One state identity over every layer at a time: the cache holds the units; structures, weather, effects, sound and figure animation are separate pure functions with separate tests and no common hash. ### Q13

> Audio delay equals distance divided by the speed of sound at the modeled temperature; attenuation and occlusion tests; silence in empty woods except documented ambient.

- **Already checked.** `app/src/audio3d/physics.mjs speedOfSound(tC) = 331.3 * sqrt(1 + tC / 273.15)`, `planner.mjs`, `atmosphere.mjs atmosphereFromWeather(wx, min)`, `terrain.mjs`, `woods.mjs`, `offline.mjs`; `app/src/audio-host.mjs`. app/tests/audio3d.test.mjs (38 tests), among them "speed of sound and delay = distance / c(T): analytic values of the plan (343 m/s: 2 km = 5.83 s; 1.5 km at 20 C = 4.4 s)", "the physics copy equals audio/model/acoustics.mjs (no drift): speed, ISO 9613-1 absorption, ISO 9613-2 ground, source levels", "ISO 9613-1 absorption reproduces the standard-table values to a few percent (20 C 70 %: 0.1 0.3 1.1 2.8 5.0 9.0 22.9 76.6 dB/km)", "spherical spreading is 6.02 dB per doubling; the planner level equals the analytic chain peak - spreading + weighted band loss", "wind: the moving-air travel time is exact (a tail wind shortens, a head wind lengthens; first order agrees)", "terrain: a 40 m ridge between source and listener casts an acoustic shadow (more loss at high frequency), a flat field casts none", "terrain: a ridge produces an echo tap whose delay is the extra path over c and whose azimuth points at the ridge", "terrain on the real DEM: ...", "ambience: crickets only at night and when warm, birds near dawn, rain only in the documented rain; thunder only in a storm; woods without events are silent of shots", "end to end (offline render): the report of a cannon starts at fire time + d/c to within one millisecond; the level follows the plan; farther is quieter and duller", "inaudible sounds are culled and counted, not made (a musket 20 km away)", "Web Audio graph (mock context): voices start at ctx.currentTime + delay, ...". audio-host.test.mjs (18 tests): "time warp: the planner keeps battle seconds (d / c), the engine divides by the speed once: the wall delay of a 2 km shot is d / (c * speed)", "offline rendering of the mix (no AudioContext): arrival = t + distance / c, channel levels and mute scale the render, music and voices are separable". audio3d-round2.test.mjs (8 tests): the woods mask ("woods-mask.json: derived from the repository 1863 woods layer (sha256 matches), tier inferred, 276 polygons, ..."). weather3d.test.mjs "Q13 input: temperature and the speed of sound at it, equal to the audio layer formula; nothing is delayed or scaled in this layer". Browser: leak.spec.mjs "sound: off until started; started inside the theatre it is one AudioContext with live nodes; Back closes it and leaves 0 nodes, 0 contexts, no timers or listeners behind".
- **Inputs present.** `environment/weather_timeline.json` (readings at 07:00, 14:00, 21:00), `app/data/audio3d/*.json`, the DEM.
- **Missing.** The scene-level statement: for the fire events the real layer emits, at sampled listeners and times, planned arrival minus emission equals distance over c(T(t)), with T(t) the temperature the weather state holds at t. Two code paths produce that temperature (`weather3d/timeline.mjs` `out.temp`, and `audio3d/atmosphere.mjs`, which falls back to `DEFAULT_TEMP_C = 20` where the record has none); their equality end to end is not tested. The planner's delay is the moving-air time d / (c + w.u); the gate says distance over the speed of sound: the stricter reading needs a ruling on the wind term. "Silence in empty woods" is tested on the ambience function, not on the real woods cells over the timeline. The real Web Audio graph is exercised against a mock context; `app/docs/BROWSER-VERIFICATION-LIST-AUDIO.md` lists the browser checks still owed.

every whole minute of a span is evaluated; a failure carries a machine code such as `unit_not_in_layer`, `unit_not_drawn`, `unit_not_on_field`, `unit_wrong_place`, `place_unknown`, `place_off_ground`, `structure_not_drawn`, `adapter_missing`), `check.mjs` (runs every record and writes `GOVERNANCE/realizations/RESULTS.json`; unknown and ambiguous names are listed, none is guessed), `lib.mjs`, `tolerances.json` (distance floors by kind of place, 20 minutes of clock tolerance, day-part windows; "a record may ask for a tighter tolerance than these; it can never ask for a wider one") and `adapters.json`. `GOVERNANCE/realizations/` holds `EXAMPLES.jsonl` (20 example records) and `RESULTS.json`, whose `q17` block read (file time 19:47): `ok` false, 691 rows, 3 realized (T1-003, T1-016, T1-019: two of kind `weather_sky`, one `structure_state`), 315 covered by an open register entry only, 373 uncovered, and of the 14 epilogue rows 12 covered by an open entry only and 2 uncovered; ### Q22

- **Already checked.** `check_environment.gate_q22()` (31 checks on the timeline itself: the 27 Jacobs readings equal the numbers parsed from the plan's Table 1, ranges, conflicts carried with both values and no chosen field, cloud fractions and wetness schedule, moonlight rows against the computed moonrise, tiers, the PENDING OWNER marking); its own text says "(No scene exists yet; this checks the timeline itself.)". `check_environment.weather_at(time_iso)` returns the governing segment and the nearest cloud readings. app/tests: unit.test.mjs "weather: states come from the weather file; gaps are said; conflicts are choices"; weather3d.test.mjs "timeline: deterministic, a pure function of t (scrub order does not matter)", "wetness and the ground: 0 until the Jul 3 storm, about 0.3 after it, 1.0 on Jul 4, drying after Jul 5; darker albedo and puddles follow it", "fog: clear days add nothing to the prototype fog; Jul 2 dawn, rain and the storm do; always tier-marked and registered", "wind: the same wind as fx3d (S to SSW, Jul 3 calm during the Charge, drift continuous); cloud drift follows it", "tier marking (Q13 inputs): every state names the tier of what it drew; weather readings are never \"documented\"; gaps are listed"; fx3d.test.mjs "wind advection: the battle-day wind blows from S to SSW, so smoke drifts north-north-east (T2-122); Jul 3 during the Charge it is nearly calm", "wind speed follows the chosen scenario, none is picked here (CF-WIND-SPEED)", "dust: dry days raise it, rain and mud do not (ground wetness from the weather file); ...", "ground impact: dry ground gives a dust cloud and hops, wet ground (Jul 4) stops the shot and gives no dust; ..."; audio3d.test.mjs "ambience: ... rain only in the documented rain; thunder only in a storm; ..."; browser ui.spec.mjs "conditions: sun and moon chips follow the sky table; a storm is named at 18:00 on 3 Jul; days without a weather record say so" (stub). The main site's weather file has its own validator (`tests/environment/weather.test.mjs`, `verify-weather.test.mjs`); re-run at 19:3x UTC (`spec-b-evidence/environment_tests.out`): `weather.test.mjs` 29 of 29 pass, `verify-weather.test.mjs` 13 of 14 fail and 1 is skipped, and its header says every test in it fails by design on the committed state of `data-src/environment/weather_by_day.json` and `tools/environment/validate.mjs`; neither reads the Battle Theatre's `environment/weather_timeline.json`.
