### 5.2 Places where the code shows a stricter reading than what is tested

1. Q04 "every scenario": seven sampled scenarios are tested.
2. Q05 "within documented ranges": upper bound only, one bound for every arm.
3. Q07, Q08, Q09 "visible", "on screen": state engines are tested exhaustively; the picture is tested for a handful of objects; most records are not drawn as geometry.
4. Q08 "gun emplacement": outside the loop of `check_q08` and outside the gate list of the G rows.
5. Q10 "at every night time ... for every unit and activity in view": ten times, one unit, placeholder layer.
6. Q11 "independent": the two methods share a Delta T polynomial and, for the moon, a common ancestor theory (the checker says so); the days 7 to 14 July rest on a JS formula compared with the table, not with a second ephemeris.
7. Q12 "state identical": identity is proven layer by layer; no hash spans all layers.
8. Q13 "distance divided by the speed of sound": the planner uses the moving-air travel time.
9. Q14 and Q01 "verified ... table": the tables in use are transcriptions of the plan's text.
10. Q17: a register link counts as coverage in `check_coverage.py`; the realization harness's `RESULTS.json` exists (3 of 691 rows realized at 19:47, 4 at 23:47) and that checker does not read it yet; epilogue rows are outside its scan, though the harness scans them.
11. Q18: 21 in the text, 22 in the data.
12. Q22 "scene weather": the existing check is of the timeline file against the plan, by its own statement.
13. Q25: status values outside the gate's three.
14. Placeholder-layer specs (`ui`, `sky`, `webgl2`, `perf`) exercise the shell, not the history.
15. Q21 "per field per date": the check function judges every unit against the data's own schedule at 100 sampled times and passes (0 failures in 555,000 states) while 539 of 925 units are plausible-procedural (538 of 927 at 00:45), the ground picture is the 2022 aerial photograph and no picture or photograph is compared; the second check (Q21.2) compares the numbers handed to the GPU with the schedule, not what the shaders draw.
16. Q16 "known camera positions": the photo-match work has 129 photographs, 105 camera-position statements and 19 poses, every pose an inference or an assumption; none is a documented position, and `photomatch-q16.test.mjs` asserts the sizes of data files that their owner was still extending.
17. Q10 "for every unit and activity in view": the WebGPU weather harness measures sky, fog, weather and effects in one stand-in view per case; the units are not in those pictures.
18. Q17 "a visual realization": the stored `RESULTS.json` is the result of the inputs of 00:42 on 14 November (digest c3bccd4ac8fdb34e), not of the tree now (1c5066a209ff3320); a pass rule that read the stored file would judge an earlier product (a scratch run at 23:00 reproduced the stored `q17` block field for field, so the numbers agree today).
19. Q16 "known camera positions": the National Park Service names three camera places (the roof of the Evergreen Cemetery lodge and the gatehouse, buildings of known outline: P27, P28, P29), and `verdicts.json` counts them as `documented_place_count` 3 beside `documented_position_count` 0; the reconciled specification and DD-PM-01 read a documented position as a coordinate with lens and plate, so the set of known positions is empty on that reading and would have three members on a looser one.
20. Q21 "within the documented-or-ranged schedule": the check function was green on the data (0 failures in 556,200 states at 23:00) while `vegetation.test.mjs` was red on 174 lines of the park-landscape corrections that the data did not account for (green at 23:27 against the rebuilt data): the function judges the data against their own schedule, not against all the evidence on file.
21. Q01.5 "arrivals record with window, place, route and sources": the runner requires place and route of every unit on the field and fails the 108 units the layer does not draw and the 25 formations above no drawn leaf (DD-G-OOB-02); `history.test.mjs` "what is drawn: only units with a printed road of approach; every drawn unit has an entry, every other leaf says why not; counts add up" only requires that the other leaves say why.
22. Q05.1 "within documented ranges": the runner bounds a declared gait from both ends (a leg declared `march` must average 1.0668 to 1.3039 m/s) and a cavalry mount by the slowest documented gallop; the tests bound only the upper end, with the foot double-quick for every mover.
23. Q14.4 and Q14.6 "within documented figures": the runner judges the fire plans the crowd driver builds against the manuals' maxima and the war establishment of 224 rounds a gun doubled (it finds a plan of 784,105 rounds from 33 pieces); the app's ballistics and fx tests check unit-level rates.
24. Q03.2 "valid on its date": the runner reads K.9 literally (every kit item has `valid_from` and `valid_to`: 248 of 249 do not) and fails the 34-star colour on and after 4 July; `check_kit.py` validates manifests and does not ask for the dates on the items.
25. Q23.3 "marked": the runner finds that the works overlay draws a documented and an inferred work alike apart from colour (the same outline pattern and glyph), which the stub-layer test "Evidence overlay: tiers are drawn as colour AND outline AND glyph" of `ui.spec.mjs` does not see.
26. Q02 and Q19 "source and tier", "independent": the runners fail every class whose Source Manifest lacks `date_written`, rights, `reliability_tier` or a recorded relation, while the app's nine contract validators report 0 problems (`contract_validators_of_the_app` in `Q02.json`): the contract does not ask for those fields.
27. Q23.7: 10 of 385 by `check_coverage.py` and 11 by `q23_marking.py` (`n/a` is an empty marking to the second): the stricter reading is the runner's.

### 5.4 What a runner must not do, from what was seen

- Run `check_environment.py` or `check_figures.py --write` as commands, or a browser spec with its default output location, without accounting for the files they rewrite (2.4 item 6; 1.1 `BT_OUT`).
- Count a browser test that printed `# NOT RUN (NEEDS_GPU)` as run.
- Count a test on the placeholder layer as evidence about the history.
- Read a red test whose assertion is a count of a data file that its owner was still extending (`photomatch-q16.test.mjs` at 00:13: 129 records against an expected 127) as a verdict on the gate or on the product: it says that test and data disagreed at that time, and it can be green again an hour later without any change in what the gate asks.
- Run a script of another workstream that rewrites its own outputs when run as a command (2.4 item 6) from a gate runner, including `tools/bt/photomatch/run.mjs`, `tools/bt/vegetation/build_vegetation.py` without `--check`, `tools/bt/kit_verified.py build` without `--out-dir` and `tools/bt/realize/check.mjs` without `--out`.
- Treat `check_structures.py` "ALL PASS" as Q07, Q08 or Q09 passing for a row: it validates the schedule and the engine, not that the product shows the row.
- Treat a stored result file (`figures-validation.json`, `results-perf.json`, `ephemeris_agreement.json`, `check_environment_results.json`) as a fresh run.
- Start more than the lock allows: the heavy items measured here are the node suite (233 s at the first run; see 7.2 for the re-check), the stage test of history.test.mjs (90 s at the first run), `gate_q11` with recomputation (31 s at the first run, 26 s at the re-check), `drill_check.py` (62 s at the first run, 45 s at the re-check), `check_figures.py` (81 s at the re-check) and the browser specs (a frame costs 4 to 5 s on this renderer: section 1.1). The lock itself can make a job wait a quarter of an hour or more: a runner starts a locked job in the background and reads its output file. At the fifth pass the wait was 54 seconds for the first locked job; two later jobs (queued at 23:06 and 23:22) had no slot after 29 and 13 minutes and were cancelled, behind 32 to 35 other jobs and two `--timing` jobs: a job that wants the lock alone makes every newcomer wait until all earlier holders have finished.
- Read the stored `GOVERNANCE/realizations/RESULTS.json` as the current state of Q17 (2.4 item 12), or treat a red test of a file that the realization workstream edits (`realize-inputs.test.mjs` test 8 at 23:0x on 15 November) as a verdict on the product.
- Count a green or red result of a test that counts or hashes a file another lane is rewriting (`roster-union-ma.test.mjs` on the Massachusetts roster, `vegetation.test.mjs` test 13 against the park-landscape corrections, `photomatch-q16.test.mjs`, the new WebGL2 test of `weather3d-gpu.test.mjs`) without the time and the digests of the files it read.
- Start `q19_claims.py` or `q23_marking.py` with `python3 -I`: both fail at the import of `q02_provenance` (2.6); start them as scripts.
- Run a runner for an experiment without `--out` (or point `--out` at `GOVERNANCE/gate_results`): the stored record and its `.prev.json` are replaced by a run on whatever the tree holds at that minute (use a scratch folder, as `pass6/run_light2.sh` does).
- Run a dense scan (`--scenarios 'S*' --grid dense`, or `q03_anachronism.py` in full) outside the lock, or from the live tree: the first is heavy (its cost for the default scenario on the dense grid is measured by locked job C of 7.8 when that job runs, and S* is a multiple of it) and the second needs about 300 s of CPU by its own docstring; the runners' author prepared frozen copies (`tmp/bt-gates/q456/snap1` and `snap2`) so that edits in the working tree do not change a run that waits for the lock.
- Read a FAIL on a unit the layer does not draw as a defect of the runner, or hide the unit to make the item pass: it is the layer's open gap (Law 7); and never read a BLOCKED-INPUT as a pass or leave it out of the verdict (it makes the verdict INCOMPLETE).
- Count a record as current when an input it hashed has changed since (`pass6/result_currency.py` compares the recorded sha256 with today's files; for `Q02.json`, at 03:30 UTC 94 of the 102 files the record hashed equal today's files; 8 differ (`library/status_3.json`, `timeline_corrections/tl-01.jsonl`, `timeline_corrections/tl-02.jsonl`, `timeline_corrections/tl-04.jsonl` and 4 more).), or compare records of different input digests.

## 6. Relation to the end-of-mission acceptance and to Section Z

- `GOVERNANCE/FINAL-ACCEPTANCE.md` (eight consecutive clean passes) has a harness for the main site: `tools/accept/run_pass.mjs`, `run_eight.mjs`, `checks/c1_hash.mjs` to `c7_visual.mjs`, `thresholds.json`, `regression_waivers.json`, logs under `docs/accept/`. Its step 6 names `tools/t0/run_suites.mjs` and `verify_release.mjs`. The folder `GOVERNANCE/final-passes/` that the file names for pass logs does not exist. Gates Q01 to Q28 are not called by that harness (a grep of `tools/accept` and `tools/t0/run_suites.mjs` for `Q01` to `Q28`, `check_governance`, `check_coverage`, `gate_results` and `GATE-HOOKS` found nothing at the re-check; five files of `tools/accept` were edited by their owner after the first writing). Re-read at the fourth pass: the file is unchanged since 4 November (4,534 bytes), `tools/accept` and `tools/t0` still contain no call of a gate or of `check_governance.py` (a text grep at 00:2x), and `tools/t0/gate.mjs` is the main site's release gate for a built train ("Release gates for a built train"), not one of Q01 to Q28; `docs/accept/README.md` was edited at 23:54 and says nothing of the Battle Theatre gates.
- The project instructions make a Battle Theatre milestone depend on the Section Z checklist and on `check_governance.py` passing. Section Z item 6 names "machine gates Q03-Q12, Q23"; items 1, 2, 4 and 7 name Q18, Q02, Q19 and Q25. `check_governance.py` passes today while those gates do not: it checks the governance files, not the gates.
- Copy audit: `tests/bt-pack/pack.test.mjs` has "copy audit of the shipped dist is clean (shipping gate; red = a source file mentions how the site was made)"; ui.spec.mjs and real.spec.mjs scan the rendered page. This file is under GOVERNANCE and is not shipped. At 00:46 UTC (the locked job of 7.6) the two pack test files (`pack.test.mjs`, `verify.test.mjs`) ran 72 tests: 69 pass, 3 fail, and the three are copy-audit tests: "the shipped kit and data files carry no production note", "copy audit of the shipped dist is clean (shipping gate; red = a source file mentions how the site was made)" and "real roster data: the printed names pass, nothing else in the 118 files is a hit (a second look at the files the roster section will ship)". The audit's hits are nine lines of the packed app (`app/data/battery-guns.json` line 3, `app/data/engagements.json` line 3, `app/data/gazetteer.json` line 1, `app/src/anim3d/gaits.mjs` line 73, `app/src/figures3d/evidence.mjs` line 2, `app/src/figures3d/skeleton.mjs` line 193, `app/src/figures3d/tiers.mjs` line 1, `app/src/history/gazetteer.mjs` line 1 and `app/src/structures/model.mjs` line 3, each a note of the kind "written by" or "built by" a tool) and one line of the roster data (`rosters/data2/INDEX.json` line 3); other workstreams' request notes under `GOVERNANCE/horde/requests/` name the same files. This is the copy-audit rule of the project instructions and of `FINAL-ACCEPTANCE.md`, not one of the gates Q01 to Q28; it is red today, and a release or a Battle Theatre milestone cannot be handed over while it is. At the fifth pass (locked job, 23:02:46 UTC, 17.3 s): the two pack test files ran 76 tests, 73 pass and 3 fail, the same three copy-audit tests with the same ten hits (the nine lines of the packed app and `rosters/data2/INDEX.json` line 3); `tests/bt-pack/pack.test.mjs` and `tools/bt/pack_theatre.mjs` are modified in the working tree by the packing lane's restart; `FINAL-ACCEPTANCE.md` is unchanged since 4 November (4,534 bytes), `tools/accept` and `tools/t0` still contain no call of a gate or of `check_governance.py` (grep at 23:1x), and the folder `GOVERNANCE/final-passes/` still does not exist.

## 7. What was run for this note

All on 2026-11-13 UTC in this container. There were two passes: the first run (18:50 to 19:13) and the re-check (from 19:18). The first run named a folder `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/spec-b-evidence/` for its raw outputs and helper scripts; that folder was never committed and was not in the tree at 19:18, so none of the first run's raw outputs survive. The re-check created the folder and re-ran what it could: every file named below is in it, next to the script or command that made it, and `light-runs.tsv` and `heavy-runs.tsv` give each command with its exit code and wall time. A figure that only the first run produced is marked FIRST RUN and is repeated as that run reported it; the re-check did not see it.

## 8. The re-verification pass (from 19:18 UTC)

### 8.1 What was checked, and how

### 8.3 What was not re-checked

The first writing's description of the content of each test beyond its title (what a test body asserts) was spot-read for the gates whose statements carry numbers (Q05, Q10, Q12, Q13, Q15, Q24) and not for every one of the 604 top-level `test()` calls counted at 19:4x UTC; the claims of section 4 about what a test "covers" are as reliable as its title and its header comment. The `Missing` bullets are judgments from reading the files and the data contract, not outputs of a program, except where a count or a file's presence is named. The statements about the real GPU (Q15, the GPU halves of Q10, Q12, Q13, Q24) are from reading the harness and cannot be verified in this container.

### 8.4 Inputs that keep arriving (how to refresh)

New inputs of other workstreams appeared during this pass at the rate of several a quarter hour (the ext layers and their tests, `kit/evidence/`, `history/undrawn/`, `tools/bt/realize/gazetteer.mjs`, `evidence_corrections/fence-1.jsonl`). The statements about absent inputs are true of the time they name. `python3 -I docs/battle-theatre/GOVERNANCE/gate_results/spec-b-evidence/facts.py` prints the live `inputs_watch` (what exists now, with sizes and first entries), the `check_*.py` scripts in the tree, the rows and verdicts of every file in `evidence_corrections/` and `timeline_corrections/` and which of the 16 pinned OCR texts are present, the `q17` block and realized rows of the realization harness's `RESULTS.json`, the DD-RZ decisions found, the files of `tests/governance` and `tools/bt/realize/` and the functions named in GATE-HOOKS.md; `implementation_facts.py` prints the test counts and data counts; `verify_quotes.py` checks that the titles in this file still exist. At the fourth pass the same refresh is `bash spec-b-evidence/pass4/run_light.sh` (37 light commands, one line each in `light-runs.tsv`, about four minutes at a load of 16; it writes `GOVERNANCE/check_report.json`, which is this workstream's own file, and nothing else outside the evidence folder and `tmp/bt-gates/`), `tools/horde/bench.sh bash spec-b-evidence/pass4/heavy_inner.sh` for the heavy items (7.6), `python3 -I spec-b-evidence/pass4/schedule_kinds.py` for the object kinds of 2.4 item 5, and `python3 -I spec-b-evidence/verify_quotes.py [DOC] [--old OLD_COPY]...` for the quotations (about 35 s of CPU: it reads 200 MB of text; run it through the lock when the machine is busy). `facts.py` now also lists, in `check_scripts`, the scripts that the other specification file keeps in `spec-a-evidence/`.

At the fifth pass the refresh is `python3 -I spec-b-evidence/pass5/inputs_delta.py [BASE]` (which inputs of each gate changed after a commit or are dirty in the working tree: 3.1), `bash spec-b-evidence/pass5/run_light.sh` (42 light commands, one line each in `light-runs.tsv`, five to six minutes at a load of 15 to 22; it writes `GOVERNANCE/check_report.json`, which is this workstream's own file, and nothing else outside the evidence folder and `tmp/bt-gates/`; `run_light_end.sh` is the same list writing to `pass5/end/`), `tools/horde/bench.sh bash spec-b-evidence/pass5/heavy_inner.sh` for the heavy items (7.7) and `heavy2_inner.sh` for the three evidence validators, `python3 -I pass5/corrections_by_gate.py` and `python3 -I pass5/library_counts.py` for the two counts that keep moving, and `python3 -I pass5/quick_quotes.py [DOC [BEFORE]]` for the quotations added or changed since a copy of the file (a few seconds, no lock; `verify_quotes.py` stays the full check).

### 8.9 The sixth pass: what was checked, and how

Method. The file as the fifth pass left it is kept as `spec-b-evidence/pass6/GATE-SPEC-B.before-pass6.md`; `pass6/build_doc.py` applies this pass to it from the text blocks of the folder and the run files, and can be run again (with `pass6/gen_sections.py` before it) when a locked job finishes. Every number added to the gate sections was read from a file or a run named in 7.8: the records of `GOVERNANCE/gate_results` (`result_digest.py`, `reason_distribution.out`), the runners' docstrings and the delegated decisions they cite, the test files by `ast` (classes and test methods), the correction files (`corrections_by_gate.py`), the data files (`facts.py`, `implementation_facts.py`), the state of the lock and the campaign log. Statements that join two files were computed, not read: that all 108 units the layer does not draw are among the 133 failures of Q01.5 (a script over `Q01.json` and `history/undrawn/status.json`: 108 of 108, and none of the 21 edge-only units), that `check_coverage.py` and `q23_marking.py` differ by one entry because of `n/a` (the register's marking of EG-0006), that `q19_claims.py` and `q23_marking.py` fail under `-I` (run), that the items of Q02, Q19 and Q23 carry no `sub_requirements` (a count over the stored records), and that the 14 mutation modules of `q456_mutations/` are the ones the three test files name. The quotation check is `pass6/quick_quotes.py` (the pass-5 program of the same name with the pass-6 file names and three changes made at this pass, described after the figures): it had not been run when this was written.

What the sixth pass found about the runners (each is repeated where it belongs): `q19_claims.py` and `q23_marking.py` fail under `python3 -I` (2.6, 2.4 item 16); Q23.7 is 10 by one checker and 11 by another (2.4 item 18, 5.2 item 27); the items of three runners name no sub-requirement (2.4 item 17); no runner reads a threshold registry and none exists (2.6); a record does not say which version of its runner made it (2.6 item 5); the end-to-end tests read the working tree (2.6 item 3); `test_q03.py` costs 128 s of CPU (2.6 item 4). It found nothing wrong with the numbers of the records themselves: the re-runs of Q23, Q19, Q02, Q01, Q14, Q20 into a scratch folder gave the verdict and the counts of their stored records; the re-run of Q25 differs from its stored record (368 items against 253), and its inputs are no longer those of the stored record: 2 differ (`library/acquired_3.json`, `library/status_3.json`), and 2 were missing then and are missing now (`library/terms.json`, `library/work_queue.json`); that is the likely reason, not a proven one, because a record does not name the version of the runner that made it (2.6).

### 8.11 What was not re-checked at the sixth pass

The test bodies beyond their titles (8.3, 8.6 and 8.8 stand); the code of the runners beyond their docstrings, their delegated decisions, their stored records and the way their tests are named (a review of the runners against the plan text is the audit step of the workflow, not this file's job); the mutation checks of Q03 and Q04 to Q06 (`mutation_check_q03.py`, `mutation_check_q456.py`) and the repeat of the one for Q01, Q14 and Q20; `gate_q11`, `gate_q22` and `drill_check.py` (their inputs did not move; the second pass's runs stand); any browser spec or harness, the capability probe included (it is the first step of locked job A, which had not started); `tools/bt/photomatch/run.mjs` and the other photo-match scripts; `mutate.mjs` and `buildlist.mjs --write` of the realization harness; `link_subreqs.py` and `register_from_proposed.py` (they write governance files); the dense runs of Q04 to Q06 over S* (their cost is the group builder's); the whole node suite (not run at this pass: its 372 s of CPU belong under a lock that had 30 jobs and two timing jobs waiting; the files that bear on a gate were run singly, 7.8); and every statement about a real GPU (from reading). The files that other lanes were rewriting while this was read, and so are true of the minute named: `kit/evidence/*.jsonl` (four files), `history/undrawn/*.jsonl`, `app/data/vegetation.json` and its module and tests, `medical/evidence/wounds-regions.jsonl` (the medical test failed on two of its rows at 02:58 UTC while its lane was extending it), `life/evidence/`, `town/evidence/`, `tools/bt/photomatch/` (`webget.py` was rewritten at 02:39), `app/tests/crowd-lod*.mjs`, `app/tests/results/sw-round-1/`, `GATE-HOOKS.md`, `horde/CAMPAIGN.md`, the correction files and the delegated-decisions file. A figure given with two times was read twice for that reason. The scratch outputs under `tmp/bt-gates/pass6` (the scratch gate records, the scratch `kit-verified` folder, the scratch `RESULTS-scratch.json`, the raw outputs of the locked jobs) are kept until this note is accepted and are not committed.
